## Stage 5-5 Cancellation / Reassignment Lifecycle — VERIFIED + 2 DEFECTS FIXED (minimal app-layer fixes only; no migration, no new architecture)
- **Status:** DONE (2026-09-07) — lifecycle matrix A–H verified against code; 2 genuine failure-path defects found and fixed; 10 new lifecycle/regression tests; 10-file focused Stage 5 suite → 107 passed / 0 failed / 0 errors / 0 skipped.
- **Raised:** 2026-09-07
- **Context:** Stage 5-5 closed the full cancellation/reassignment matrix (A coordinator cancel, B attendee cancel, C accommodation-side cancel not propagated, D repeated-cancel idempotency, E atomic reassignment, F cancel→reassign, G reassign→cancel, H cross-context rejection). Matrix A–H held in code via the Stage 5-4 primitives (contract slot release, single-commit pointer+token clear, fail-closed retire, event-side cancel). The inspection surfaced TWO unguarded `release_event_guest_slot` call sites related to a missing/deleted OLD booking.
- **What was fixed (both in `app/events/guest_coordination_service.py`):** (D1) coordinator `cancel()` at the accommodation branch — the old-booking slot release raised an unguarded `CoordinationContractError("BOOKING_NOT_FOUND")` when the booking had been soft-deleted, surfacing as an HTTP 500. Now wrapped: skip only `BOOKING_NOT_FOUND` (log + continue), re-raise others — matching the established skip pattern in `cancel_attendee_booking`/`retire_invalid_accommodation_assignment`. (D2) reassignment path — the old-booking slot release similarly raised `BOOKING_NOT_FOUND`, aborting an otherwise valid reassignment to a new booking with `COORDINATION_FAILED` (500). Now wrapped identically: skip only `BOOKING_NOT_FOUND` (log + continue) so the reassignment completes. Both changes are pure Python; `current_app` was already imported in the file. No schema, no migration, no MODULE_FLAGS, no architecture change.
- **Tests:** NEW `tests/test_stage5b5_cancellation_reassignment_lifecycle.py` → **10 passed / 0 failed**: A–H matrix (test_a…test_h) + 2 regression tests (`test_d1_coordinator_cancel_survives_deleted_old_booking`, `test_d2_reassignment_survives_deleted_old_booking`). Combined Stage 5 focus suite (test_assignment_ownership_boundary, test_stage5b2_assignment_lifecycle, test_stage5b3_handoff_architecture, test_guest_coordination_accommodation, test_guest_coordination_contract, test_accommodation_lifecycle_verification, test_guest_assignment_account_optional, test_event_accommodation_assignment_flow, test_stage5b4_sync_synchronization, test_stage5b5_cancellation_reassignment_lifecycle) → **107 passed / 0 failed / 0 errors / 0 skipped** (was 97 in 9 files at Stage 5-4; +10 from the new file).
- **Verification:** startup `python -c "from app import create_app"` → exit 0, `STARTUP_OK`; migration head single **`1788729103`** unchanged; `flask db current` = `1788729103 (head)`; no migration created/applied; no `MODULE_FLAGS` writes; no schema change; no wallet/payment/escrow/transport work; AUTO-STOP honored after this record.

## Legacy accommodation reminder notification types are silently dropped
- **Status:** RESOLVED — 2026-09-09 (Option B: call sites conformed to canonical enum; no migration, no CHECK-constraint sync)
- **Raised:** 2026-09-07
- **Context:** While implementing the pre-arrival request reminder (AFCON360_OUTSTANDING_TASKS Task 5), discovery showed `app/tasks/accommodation_reminders.py` `_send_notification()` passes legacy type strings (`registration_reminder_72h`, etc.) to `NotificationService.send`. `Notification.type` is a String column with an enum-generated CHECK constraint `ck_notifications_type`; `NotificationType(value)` raises `ValueError`, and `NotificationService.send` swallows exceptions and returns `None` (services.py:424-427). Net effect: the legacy reminders NEVER create notifications (silent failure). The new Task 5 task avoids this by using the real enum value `NotificationType.BOOKING_UPDATE` with an explicit `module=NotificationModule.ACCOMMODATION`.
- **What needs to happen (chosen): (b) rewrite legacy call sites to real enum values.** Done — `app/tasks/accommodation_reminders.py`: `registration_reminder_72h/48h/24h/post_booking` and `registration_deadline_passed` → `NotificationType.BOOKING_UPDATE`; the shared `_send_notification()` now passes `module=NotificationModule.ACCOMMODATION` (required — `booking_update` is deliberately absent from `TYPE_MODULE_FALLBACK`). Beneficial side-effect: `booking_expired` / `booking_no_show` reminders (valid enum values) are now correctly tagged `accommodation` instead of falling back to `system`. Live-DB probe (read-only, `afcon360_prod`) confirmed zero legacy-type rows (102 canonical rows) and enforced `ck_notifications_type`, so no data migration needed. Option (a) (enum expansion) rejected: would require §20.2 CHECK sync + per-type templates + broadens accepted values. `NotificationService.send` invalid-type handling intentionally unchanged (swallow-and-log is the established best-effort contract; changing it is out of scope).
- **VERIFICATION:** `pytest tests/notifications/test_accommodation_reminders.py` → 2 passed (call-site compliance spy over `send_registration_reminders` asserting all 4 reminder types = `BOOKING_UPDATE` + `module=ACCOMMODATION`; persistence test asserting a real `Notification` row lands with type `booking_update` / module `accommodation`). Full `pytest tests/notifications/` → 60 passed, 5 failed — the 5 failures are ONLY the separately-tracked pre-existing `user_id=1` preference-collision defect in `test_models.py` (proven identical on pristine baseline via `git stash`; see BACKLOG "notifications test hardcodes user_id=1"). Startup `python -c "from app import create_app"` → `STARTUP_OK`. No migration created/applied; no schema change.
- **DEFERRED (recorded, NOT fixed; dormant + ambiguous mapping):** `notify_incomplete_properties` (`_send_property_notification` at `app/tasks/accommodation_reminders.py:407`) still passes legacy `"property_incomplete"` type. Task is registered but NOT in Celery beat (dormant); `property_incomplete` has no clear canonical accommodation enum value, so mapping it to `BOOKING_UPDATE` would invent a contract. Recommend a dedicated node/decision before fixing. Also unchanged: `NotificationService.send` silent-swallow design.
- **Owner/area:** notifications + accommodation tasks
- **Links:** app/tasks/accommodation_reminders.py (_send_notification), app/notifications/services.py:424-427, app/notifications/models.py (NotificationType / ck_notifications_type), tests/notifications/test_accommodation_reminders.py, §20.2, §26

The actual live code CONTRADICTS this: `AvailabilityService.create_hold()` (availability_service.py:401-509) DOES instantiate `RoomHold` (line 484) and uses `InventoryBlock` (room-type scoped) for multi-unit properties, falling back to property-wide `BlockedDate` only for single-unit. No code change.

## Property `booking_mode` is never persisted by host create/edit flows
- **Status:** RESOLVED (2026-09-08) — booking_mode now persisted by host create/update; focused regression tests added
- **Raised:** 2026-09-07
- **Context:** Discovery during the `edit_listing.html` restyle (template-only change; documented in `static/MOBILE_OPTIMIZATION.md` §0.1 follow-up): both `create_listing.html` and `edit_listing.html` render `form.booking_mode` (`PropertyForm` at `app/accommodation/forms.py:234`, values `instant` / `host_approval`), and `booking_service.py:186` branches on `property.booking_mode == 'host_approval'` — but **nothing persists it**. `host_service.create_property()` (line ~161) and `update_property()` (line ~254) write `instant_book` only; the `booking_mode` column (`app/accommodation/models/property.py:219`, default `"instant"`) is never written by these flows. The edit route (`routes.py:4641` `form.process(...)`) does not pre-populate `booking_mode` either. Pre-existing (identical on create and edit, present before the restyle); template-only fix is NOT the right remediation.
- **Root cause:** `HostService.create_property()` (create) and `HostService.update_property()` (update) persisted `instant_book` but never assigned `Property.booking_mode`. Since the model defaults `booking_mode` to `"instant"` (`nullable=False`), a host selecting `"host_approval"` had the value silently dropped and the property stayed `"instant"`, so `booking_service.py:186` never routed bookings to the host-approval flow.
- **Correction (2026-09-08, minimal):** `app/accommodation/services/host_service.py` — `create_property()` now sets `booking_mode=(data.get("booking_mode") or "instant")`; `update_property()` now sets `prop.booking_mode = data.get("booking_mode") or prop.booking_mode` (preserves existing when the key is omitted on edit). No schema/CHECK-constraint change — `booking_mode` is `String(20)`, no `ck_*` constraint — so no migration and no `sync_check_constraints.py` run needed.
- **Tests added:** `tests/test_booking_mode_persistence.py` (5 tests): create persists `host_approval` and `instant`; omitted defaults to `instant`; update persists mode change; update keeps existing mode when omitted.
- **Verification:** focused `tests/test_booking_mode_persistence.py` -> 5 passed; accommodation regression (`test_org10_adversarial_cross_property.py`, `test_accommodation_capacity_rules.py`) -> 17 passed with 1 pre-existing unrelated fail (`test_accommodation_roomtype.py` bookable-Property fixture gap); targeted recurrence-across suite (`test_booking_mode_persistence.py` + `test_org9_public_boundaries.py` + `test_stage4b6_org_write_gate.py` + `test_accommodation_lifecycle_verification.py` + `test_event_accommodation_assignment_flow.py` + `test_accommodation_checkout_processes.py`) -> 88 passed, 0 failed.
- **Owner/area:** accommodation services + routes + forms
- **Links:** app/accommodation/forms.py:234, app/accommodation/models/property.py:219, app/accommodation/services/host_service.py (create ~161, update ~254), app/accommodation/routes.py:4641/4668, app/accommodation/services/booking_service.py:186, templates/accommodation/host/{create,edit}_listing.html, static/MOBILE_OPTIMIZATION.md §0.1, tests/test_booking_mode_persistence.py

## Pre-existing flaky test: `test_accommodation_booking.py::test_concurrent_last_room_booking` (idempotency race)
- **Status:** RESOLVED (PRODUCTION DEFECT — fixed 2026-09-09). Prior classification "flaky test / advisory-lock semantics" was WRONG; repro against PRISTINE production code always produced ONE caller getting `(None, "Dates ... not available")` while the other succeeded and `db_count==1` (no overbooking, single row) — a genuine contract violation: equivalent concurrent duplicate requests did not both return the same booking. Dynamic instrumentation (`[KDIAG-*]` step logs inside `create_booking`: enter/lock/idem/avail/commit with backend PID per thread) PROVED the interleaving 6/6 stress iterations: the loser's `pg_advisory_xact_lock` returned ~200ms BEFORE the winner's final commit — because `AvailabilityService.block_dates` (`availability_service.py:396`) and `AvailabilityService.block_room_type_units` (`availability_service.py:776`) each call `db.session.commit()` INSIDE the advisory-lock critical section, releasing the xact lock before the booking row commits. Delta: winner commits inventory blocks (mid-transaction commit) → loser acquires lock (no holder) → loserdempotency check misses winner's uncommitted booking → loser availability check now sees winner's committed blocks → returns "Dates ... not available". `pg_advisory_xact_lock` itself verified CORRECT via micro-experiment (waiter blocked 2.47s on raw connections; both failing threads had DISTINCT backend PIDs — not same-session). MINIMAL FIX (PRODUCTION, no concurrency redesign, no test/assertion change): at the two availability-failure exits inside the protected region (`booking_service.py` `Only N units available` and `not is_range_available` branches), re-check idempotency (same key + guest_user_id) and return the now-existing booking before returning the error — mirroring the existing IntegrityError fallback pattern (`booking_service.py:~660`). Verifications: deterministic 6-iteration stress probe: 6/6→0/6 failures; focused pytest `test_concurrent_last_room_booking`: 4/4 full pytest passes (plus 1 pre-fix focused pass in earlier runs was flake window); idempotency neighbors `test_create_booking_idempotency` + `test_create_booking_idempotency_concurrent` + last-room: 3 passed (×2 separate runs); regression subset (full_instant_book_flow, request_to_book_flow, capacity_rules, checkout_processes, date_range_rules, roomtype availability): 16 passed / 1 failed where the 1 fail = the SEPARATE pre-existing `"blocked" vs "booked"` roomtype snapshot-classification ACTIVE item (`BACKLOG:998`) — re-verified failing on PRISTINE HEAD (my change stashed), NOT a regression. `python -c "from app import create_app..."` → STARTUP_OK. No migration, no test-file change, no assertion weakened; temp probes removed. DEFERRED hardening (not done — concurrency redesign, out of scope): hold the advisory guard until the booking commit by removing the inner `db.session.commit()` from `block_dates`/`block_room_type_units` (callers' transaction owns commit) or switching to a session-level lock; note the same inner-commit pattern at other early-return error branches is covered by the idempotency re-checks but different-key concurrent losers still correctly get the availability error (inventory safety preserved).
- **Raised:** 2026-09-08
- **Context:** During ORG-9/ORG-10 closure verification, this threading test failed in a combined run (`assert len(successful) == 2` got 1 — only one of the two threads returned the same idempotent booking). Reproduced on the PRISTINE tree (my ORG-9/ORG-10 changes stashed; test untouched by the boundaries work). Previously PASSED in an isolated earlier run (see session log) — the two threads each open their own `create_app()` + `app_context()` and race on a shared fixture booking; completion is scheduling-dependent when a slow thread loses the barrier wake-up / idempotency lookup timing. Classified PRE-EXISTING / flaky concurrency, NOT an ORG-9/ORG-10 regression. Per §9/§34 documented, NOT silently fixed (no authorization; test relies on real advisory-lock concurrency semantics).
- **What needs to happen:** When next stabilising the test suite: make the two worker threads deterministic (e.g. barrier inside the context before `create_booking`, or assert `len(successful) >= 1` plus identical booking id when a duplicate is present) OR fix the actual race — requires deciding the intended idempotency guarantee under overlapping duplicate submissions before touching the test. Separate from the roomtype/attendee fixture gaps already recorded elsewhere in this file.
- **Owner/area:** tests / accommodation booking service
- **Links:** tests/test_accommodation_booking.py::test_concurrent_last_room_booking (~line 740-780), app/accommodation/services/booking_service.py:288-317 (advisory lock + idempotency check)

## FUTURE IMPLEMENTATION — NOT STARTED: transaction-boundary cleanup for inner commits inside the booking critical section
- **Status:** FUTURE IMPLEMENTATION — NOT STARTED
- **Raised:** 2026-09-09
- **Context:** During the RESOLVED `test_concurrent_last_room_booking` investigation (see entry directly above), the root cause was PROVEN to be `db.session.commit()` calls performed INSIDE `create_booking`'s advisory-lock critical section: `AvailabilityService.block_dates` (`app/accommodation/services/availability_service.py:396`) and `AvailabilityService.block_room_type_units` (`availability_service.py:776`) each commit while the `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (a xact-scope lock, `booking_service.py:292`) is still logically held. Any inner commit terminates the transaction and releases the xact lock BEFORE the booking row is committed, so a concurrent same-key duplicate can pass the idempotency check (winner uncommitted) and then fail availability after the winner's blocks are visible. The current fix (idempotency re-check at the availability-failure exits, `booking_service.py`) closes the observed contract violation WITHOUT touching transaction boundaries.
- **Future task (deferred by design — concurrency/transaction redesign, NOT a defect to fix silently):** review and potentially redesign the inner transaction commits so the `pg_advisory_xact_lock` critical section remains within ONE transaction from lock acquisition to the booking commit at `booking_service.py:622`. Candidate directions (each needs its own planning/spec/GATE before implementation): (a) remove the commit from `block_dates`/`block_room_type_units` and let the caller's transaction own the commit — verify every caller (incl. `create_hold`, hold-release/release paths) still ends with a commit so no partial state is written; (b) replace the xact lock with a session-level `pg_advisory_lock(...)` + explicit release at the end of the booking flow, verifying lock lifetime and the risk of leaked session locks; (c) keep the current behavior and document inner-commit releases as intentional, relying on the idempotency re-checks as the correctness net. All call sites of `block_dates` / `block_room_type_units` must be traced and their commit expectations verified before any change.
- **Explicitly retained (intentional):** the current RESOLVED concurrency fix (idempotency re-check at the two availability-failure branches in `booking_service.py`) is intentionally kept; no production code, schema, migration, or tests were changed by this cleanup node.
- **Owner/area:** accommodation / booking + availability services (transaction boundaries)
- **Links:** app/accommodation/services/booking_service.py:292 (advisory lock),:622 (booking commit); app/accommodation/services/availability_service.py:348-398 (`block_dates`),:740-782 (`block_room_type_units`); BACKLOG entry above (resolved concurrent last-room node)

## Booking-mode / roomtype test-integrity reconciliation (bookable-Property gap CLEARED; 3 failures remain, classified)
- **Status:** Partially resolved (2026-09-08) - test-only changes; no production code, no migration
- **Raised:** 2026-09-08
- **Context:** Reopened the family tracked at BACKLOG:79 (reconciliation 2026-09-08 items (i)/(ii)), 4B-6 deferred item (b) (BACKLOG:775-776), and 4B-7 failure-family item (10) (BACKLOG:788): `tests/test_accommodation_roomtype.py` + `tests/test_booking_mode.py` bookable-Property fixture gap and downstream failures.
- **CLEARED - bookable-Property fixture gap (Item #5 root cause):** the fixtures/domain state created non-bookable properties (`status`/`is_verified`/`is_active` not set to bookable values) so `Property.can_be_booked()` (`app/accommodation/models/property.py:498-502`) returned False and `BookingService.create_booking` failed with "Property is not available for booking". PRODUCTION gate was CORRECT; the tests were the wrong layer (§9). Fix (TEST-ONLY): `tests/test_booking_mode.py` fixtures `instant_property`/`host_approval_property` now set `is_verified=True, verification_status="verified"`; `tests/test_accommodation_roomtype.py::test_available_units_and_booking_creation` now promotes the property through the moderation-approval fields (`status="published"`, `is_verified=True`, `verification_status="verified"`, `is_active=True`). Verified: booking creation succeeds everywhere; no "Property is not available for booking" failure remains anywhere.
- **CORRECTED - host-approval payment test wrong-layer (wrong TEST vs spec):** `tests/test_booking_mode.py::test_host_approval_payment_received_stays_pending_approval` asserted `confirm_booking` succeeds (`success=True`, `payment_status=PAID`) while status stays PENDING_APPROVAL. That contradicts the approved Request-to-Book flow `Implement/booking_flow.md:105` (`HELD -> PENDING_APPROVAL -> (host accepts) -> PENDING_PAYMENT -> CONFIRMED` — approval precedes payment) and production `BookingPolicyEvaluator.can_confirm` (`app/accommodation/state_machine/policy_evaluator.py:91-99` gate evaluated BEFORE payment; `booking_service.py:1284-1286` returns `(False, "Host approval required before confirmation")`; no payment captured). Sibling passing test `test_host_approval_booking_requires_payment_and_approval` correctly tolerates the blocked confirm. Fix (TEST-ONLY, strengthened): test now asserts `success is False`, error contains "Host approval required", status stays PENDING_APPROVAL, and `payment_status` stays PENDING (payment NOT captured pre-approval).
- **ACTIVE (new this node - roomtype snapshot classification, NOT a fixture gap):** `tests/test_accommodation_roomtype.py::test_available_units_and_booking_creation` asserts `day["status"] == "booked"` at `:251` but `get_property_calendar_snapshot` (`app/accommodation/services/host_service.py:1420-1526`) returns `"blocked"`/`blocked_reason="temporary_hold"` when avl = 0: the snapshot day-classification only counts `ACTIVE_BOOKING_STATUSES = ['confirmed','checked_in','pending']` (`host_service.py:30-34`) bookings, and the `pending_payment` booking's inventory is held by a `temporary_hold` InventoryBlock (blocked, not booked). Production display semantics are plausible (a pending/hold day is a block); no canonical display contract found — needs a decision (test-side correction to accept `blocked` OR production change to include `pending_payment`/`pending_approval` holds in the "booked" determination, WITH spec evidence). Recorded, NOT auto-fixed.
  - **Reconciliation (2026-09-10):** RESOLVED — **TEST DEFECT (classification A)**. Trace proved the hold contract: `BookingService.create_booking` creates a `temporary_hold` InventoryBlock for the PENDING_PAYMENT booking (`booking_service.py:540-549`) and converts it to reason=`booked` only when payment verifies (`:506-533`); `available_units` and the snapshot both count `InventoryBlock.reason != 'booked'` (`host_service.py:1622-1631`, `:1501-1506`). Authoritative contract = `Implement/booking_flow.md`: HELD/PENDING_PAYMENT is the "Commitment" phase, CONFIRMED is "Contract" (`:100-101`); inventory lifecycle `AVAILABLE → HELD → BOOKED → AVAILABLE` (`:534`); availability formula deducts `booked - held - out_of_service` separately (`:227`). Consumers expect the distinction: host calendar renders `blocked_reason` ("temporary hold") and treats only `booked` days as permanently reserved (`templates/accommodation/host/calendar.html:589-651`), the dashboard surfaces active `temporary_hold` blocks (`routes.py:4262-4276`), and the manual-block route rejects `{booked, temporary_hold}` as system-reserved reasons (`routes.py:4867-4869`). A held (not confirmed) day is therefore correctly `"blocked"`; the failing assertion was the wrong expectation. **MINIMAL CHANGE (test-only):** `tests/test_accommodation_roomtype.py:244-257` — corrected the stale comment and `:251` asserts `day["status"] == "blocked"` with `day["blocked_reason"] in ("temporary_hold", "MAINTENANCE")` (the day's `temporary_hold` + 2×`MAINTENANCE` InventoryBlocks are unordered). Availability math assertions (5→4→2→0) unchanged. **Verification:** `tests/test_accommodation_roomtype.py` → 2 passed; regression `tests/test_booking_mode.py tests/test_booking_mode_persistence.py tests/test_accommodation_booking.py tests/test_accommodation_checkout_processes.py` → **53 passed**. No production code, no migration, no other test changed. Only snapshot day-status assertions in the suite are in this file (coverage complete).
- **CLEARED (2026-09-09) - DB-pollution family (guest2/guest3 static-email collisions):** `tests/test_booking_mode.py` availability tests used hardcoded emails `guest2@example.com` (`test_unavailable_property_cannot_be_instant_booked`) and `guest3@example.com` (`test_host_approval_also_validates_availability`, tracked from BACKLOG:79(ii)); these collided with committed rows on the persistent `afcon360_test` DB (`ValueError: Duplicate email found` at `app/identity/models/user.py:887`), NOT availability logic. Mechanism PROVEN (§9): conftest reuses the DB whenever `users` exists (`tests/conftest.py:188-189`, no truncation); the two test bodies explicitly `db.session.commit()` the static-email users (`test_booking_mode.py:412,456`); function-scoped teardown only rolls back uncommitted changes; probe confirmed persisted rows id 3889 (`guest2@example.com`) and 2480 (`guest3@example.com`) already in `afcon360_test`; tests failed alone, in-module, and in-suite (deterministic — not ordering). Production uniqueness guard (`app/identity/models/user.py:889-892`: email/username/phone/public_id) is CORRECT. Fix (TEST-ONLY): the two second-guest creations now use unique `email` AND `username` per run via `f"guest{2|3}-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:6]}@example.com"` — the same established pattern as the module's own `guest_user`/`host_user` fixtures. Availability assertions unchanged (second booking for the same dates must still fail). Verified: both tests PASS alone (2 passed), full module `tests/test_booking_mode.py` -> **12 passed**, regression `tests/test_booking_mode_persistence.py tests/test_accommodation_checkout_processes.py tests/test_accommodation_roomtype.py` -> **16 passed / 1 failed** (the 1 fail = the separate `"blocked" vs "booked"` snapshot-classification finding, NOT a regression). No other test uses the static `guestN@example.com` pattern (grep clean). No production code changed, no migration.
- **NEW spec-vs-code discrepancy (BEHAVIORAL, decision required, NOT fixed):** production `BookingService.approve_booking` (`booking_service.py:1417-1424`) transitions PENDING_APPROVAL -> CONFIRMED directly WITHOUT the spec's intermediate `PENDING_PAYMENT` step (`Implement/booking_flow.md:105`). This means a host-approval booking is confirmed with no payment capture. The two passing tests that assert approve->CONFIRMED (`test_host_can_approve_pending_booking`, `test_host_approval_booking_requires_payment_and_approval`) do NOT exercise payment. Needs a product/spec decision: align `approve_booking` to `PENDING_PAYMENT` before CONFIRMED, or accept confirm-without-payment for host-approval bookings as the intended contract.
- **Side observation (out of scope):** `booking_service.py:~632` notification path logs `NameError: name 'requires_host_approval' is not defined` (caught, dev-only warning) — latent bug, NOT part of this reconciliation.
- **Verification:** focused `tests/test_booking_mode.py tests/test_accommodation_roomtype.py` -> **11 passed, 3 failed** (was 13 failed at node start; the 3 = the ACTIVE items above, all classified, none a regression from this work). Regression `tests/test_booking_mode_persistence.py tests/test_accommodation_checkout_processes.py` -> **15 passed**. No production code or migration touched. Working tree otherwise preserved.
- **Owner/area:** tests / accommodation booking + host service
- **Links:** tests/test_booking_mode.py, tests/test_accommodation_roomtype.py, app/accommodation/models/property.py:498-502, app/accommodation/services/booking_service.py:233-236,1209-1286,1391-1472, app/accommodation/state_machine/policy_evaluator.py:91-99, app/accommodation/services/host_service.py:30-34,1420-1526, app/identity/models/user.py:887, Implement/booking_flow.md:105, BACKLOG:79,775-776,788

All meaningful assertions preserved.
  2. `test_get_booking_requirements` — **Production (response-contract) defect — per user decision**: the with-booking branch of `AttendeeAccommodationBookingService.get_booking_requirements` returned the raw `BookingPolicyEvaluator.get_booking_requirements(booking)` dict without the `has_booking` key, while the same method's no-booking branch returns `{"has_booking": False, ...}` (both shipped in commit `fbc7491`; no template/route/test consumes the raw shape). Decision (user, 2026-09-09, Booker-Guest-Payer clarification): `has_booking` is a booking-existence fact owned by the attendee service contract; the evaluator stays unchanged. Fix (1 additive line): `requirements["has_booking"] = True` before returning. `BookingPolicyEvaluator` NOT modified.
  3. `test_cancel_attendee_booking` — **Test defect (missing fixture parameter)**: test body used `test_organizer_user.id` but the fixture was not requested in the signature (only transitively via `test_event`), causing `AttributeError: 'FixtureFunctionDefinition' object has no attribute 'id'`. Fix: added `test_organizer_user` to the test signature (fixture reuse, no DB change).
  4–6. `test_available_properties_api`, `test_book_accommodation_api`, `test_book_accommodation_pay_on_arrival_api` — **Test defect (wrong Flask-Login session key)**: tests set `sess['user_id']` but the routes use `@login_required`, which reads `session['_user_id']` = `str(user.public_id)` (Flask-Login user_loader query on public_id). Fix: set `sess['_user_id'] = str(attendee_registration.user.public_id)` (`_fresh=True` kept). Additionally, `test_book_accommodation_requires_auth` now explicitly clears `_user_id`/`user_id`/`_fresh` first — the `client` fixture is session-scoped, so the three logging-in tests would otherwise leave the cookie set and make the no-login test order-dependent. No production change.

## DB-pollution family — persisted-MODULE_FLAGS contamination — ROOT CAUSE FIXED (resolves Stage 5-1 deferral (a))
- **Status:** RESOLVED/CLEARED (2026-09-09) — the pollution SOURCE is fixed, so the event-accommodation family is deterministic on the shared test DB; this is the "clear ONE concrete unresolved DB-pollution sub-item" node. Supersedes the manual/operator remedy recorded in Stage 5-1 deferral (a) (BACKLOG:825-826) — the DB row no longer needs to be deleted manually because no test recreates it.
- **Raised:** tracked since 2026-09-06 (Stage 5-1: `tests/test_event_accommodation_assignment_flow.py` 4 failures — `test_normal_shared_booking_capacity`, `test_event_assignment_completion_at_capacity`, `test_reassignment_deactivates_old_slot`, `test_cancellation_frees_capacity` — caused by a persisted `SystemConfig.MODULE_FLAGS` override `{"tourism": true, "transport": false, "accommodation": false}` in `afcon360_test`; classified B-class test-DB state pollution).
- **UNDERSTAND/MAP:** the override is applied at request time by `app/middleware/reload_modules.py` (`@app.before_request` → `ModuleToggleService.load_overrides_into_app()`) and by `GuestCoordinationService._module_available` (`app/utils/module_guard.module_enabled` reads `current_app.config["MODULE_FLAGS"]`); with accommodation disabled the affected tests raised `CoordinationError: ('ACCOMMODATION_UNAVAILABLE', ...)`. `tests/conftest.py` reuses `afcon360_test` when `users` exists (no truncation, rollback-only `clean_db`), so a committed override persists across runs.
- **PROVE — root cause (B-class TEST isolation/fixture defect, OUTPUT of the polluter):** `tests/test_module_integration.py::ModuleIsolationIntegrationTest` (a `unittest.TestCase` that pytest collects because unittest subclasses are collected regardless of the `Test*` prefix) WRITES the flags through `ModuleToggleService.set_flag(...)` which COMMITS to the shared DB (module_toggle_service.py:79) and NEVER restored them: `test_6_dashboard_with_disabled_modules` sets tourism/transport/accommodation to False; `test_7_module_reload_middleware` toggles tourism back to True. Alphabetical order reproduces EXACTLY the documented stored value. Reproduced deterministically: (1) `pytest tests/test_module_integration.py -q` → 8 passed AND probe showed `MODULE_FLAGS STORED: {"tourism": true, "transport": false, "accommodation": false}` (was None before); (2) the 4 event-accommodation tests then FAILED 4/4 with `ACCOMMODATION_UNAVAILABLE`; (3) after deleting only the row that my own proof-created (restoring the pre-experiment None baseline), the same 4 tests PASS. Production toggle behavior is CORRECT (a genuinely disabled module MUST fail accommodation flows — `@module_required`/`module_enabled` gates are the intended contract, see §28); the genetic fault was the test dropping durable state.
- **MINIMAL CHANGE (TEST-ONLY):** `tests/test_module_integration.py` — `setUp` now snapshots the persisted `SystemConfig["MODULE_FLAGS"]` (row-present flag + stored dict) and a new `tearDown` restores it (delete row if it was absent, else write the snapshot back), then `ModuleToggleService.load_overrides_into_app()`. Mirrors the established save/restore pattern already used by `test_org10_adversarial_cross_property.py:278-306`, `test_stage4b2_capability_enforcement.py:114-152`, `test_stage4b6_org_write_gate.py:403-431`. No production code, no migration, no assertion change, no sleeps/retries, no DB-row deletion used as a test workaround.
- **Verification:**
  - Polluter determinism: `pytest tests/test_module_integration.py -q` → **8 passed**, and afterwards `MODULE_FLAGS STORED: None` (DB left clean — the prior run WITHOUT the fix left the documented polluted value).
  - Focused (the 4 originally-failing tests under the polluted state): with the pollution REINTRODUCED they failed 4/4 with `ACCOMMODATION_UNAVAILABLE`; with the pollution source fixed + clean DB they pass (contained in the 19 below).
  - Full containing event-accommodation family: `tests/test_event_accommodation_assignment_flow.py` + `tests/test_guest_coordination_accommodation.py` → **19 passed / 0 failed**.
  - Relevant regression (module-toggle consumers + capability/org flip-restore suites + event-accommodation neighbors): `tests/test_module_isolation.py tests/test_module_integration_simple.py tests/test_live_module_isolation.py tests/test_stage4b2_capability_enforcement.py tests/test_stage4b6_org_write_gate.py tests/test_org9_public_boundaries.py tests/test_org10_adversarial_cross_property.py tests/test_assignment_ownership_boundary.py tests/test_stage5b3_handoff_architecture.py tests/test_attendee_accommodation_booking.py` → **131 passed / 0 failed / 0 errors / 0 skipped**.
  - DB state after all verification: `MODULE_FLAGS STORED: None` (clean).
  - Startup: `python -c "from app import create_app; app=create_app(); print('STARTUP_OK')"` → `STARTUP_OK`, exit 0.
- **Scope gate:** one DB-pollution sub-item only; Wallet/Payment/Escrow untouched; Stage 6 untouched; Transport NOT fixed (only its shared module-flag row no longer gets re-polluted); Notification untouched; concurrent last-room booking untouched; `setup_test_db_schema.py` untouched (not run, not fixed); no assertion weakened; no broad refactor; no migration created/applied.
- **Remaining active in DB-pollution family:** **RESOLVED (2026-09-09): Notification `user_id=1` collision** — the `UserNotificationPreference` unique-index (`idx_unp_user_type_channel`) collisions in `tests/notifications/test_models.py`; see the dedicated node entry below. **RESOLVED (2026-09-09): Transport email collision (`test_transport_passengers`)** — hardcoded static-email User creation removed; see the dedicated node entry below. Stream to track separately: **Transport license-plate collision (stage5t3) — RESOLVED (2026-09-09)**, recorded node below. Stage 5-1 deferral (b) (coordination-file docstring/slot reconciliation) is a documentation item, NOT DB pollution — still active, separate.

## Transport passenger hardcoded-user collision (test_transport_passengers) — RESOLVED
- **Status:** RESOLVED (2026-09-09).
- **Raised:** observed 2026-09-08 as the single pre-existing failure in the transport reconciliation set ("1 pre-existing fail = test_transport_passengers email collision, unrelated", BACKLOG:642); documented as an excluded DB-pollution family above (line 1055).
- **UNDERSTAND/PROVE (TEST/FIXTURE DEFECT, same DB-pollution family):** `tests/test_transport_passengers.py` created REAL `User` rows with STATIC hardcoded emails — `test_claim_token_single_use_and_invalidation` (`email="rider@example.com"`, line 215) and `test_claim_token_recipient_binding` (`email="someone-else@example.com"`, line 260). `users.email` is UNIQUE (`user.py:83`); `User.before_insert` (`app/identity/models/user.py:866-899`) raises `ValueError: Duplicate email found: <email>` whenever a committed row with that email already exists in the shared persistent `afcon360_test` DB (which retains committed rows across runs; function-scoped rollback does not undo commits). Failure form CONFIRMED via a self-cleaning in-transaction pytest probe (`Duplicate email found: rider@example.com`, rolled back — zero persistent change). All other `rider@example.com` uses in the file are accountless `transport_passengers` snapshot rows (no unique constraint; 8 such rows in DB are normal). DB probe: 0 `users` rows for either email before AND after the fix.
- **MINIMAL CHANGE (TEST-ONLY, 2 lines):** (1) `test_claim_token_single_use_and_invalidation` — `rider = _user(db_session, "rider")` (UUID-unique email) and `p = svc.add_passenger(b, name="Rider", email=rider.email)` (preserves claim recipient-binding premise: user email == passenger email); (2) `test_claim_token_recipient_binding` — `wrong = _user(db_session, "wrong")` (UUID-unique email; the `wrong-` prefix can never equal `rider@example.com`, so the mismatch → `PermissionError` premise is unchanged). No production code, no migration, no assertion change, no DB cleanup, no browser/sleep workarounds.
- **Verification:** targeted 2 passed; full `tests/test_transport_passengers.py` → **18 passed / 0 failed**; reconciliation set (`test_transport_stage5t4.py test_transport_passengers.py test_transport_coordination_contract.py test_transport_stage5t3.py`) → all 18 email-family tests passed (the set's single error is the separate license-plate defect recorded below); `python -c "from app import create_app"` → clean.
- **Owner/area:** tests / transport passengers–claim/link (DB-pollution family)
- **Links:** tests/test_transport_passengers.py:213-232,257-268, app/identity/models/user.py:83,866-899, BACKLOG:642, AGENTS.md §9, §12.1, §19.3, §23, §38, §39

## Notification preference user_id=1 collision (test_models.py) — RESOLVED
- **Status:** RESOLVED (2026-09-09).
- **Raised:** observed as the 5 pre-existing failures in `tests/notifications/test_models.py` across suite runs (see "Full-suite failure classification" and the notification regression entries); documented as the excluded DB-pollution family (BACKLOG:732-742, :1055).
- **UNDERSTAND/PROVE (TEST/FIXTURE DEFECT, same DB-pollution family):** the 5 failing tests all INSERT + COMMIT a `UserNotificationPreference` with the SAME hardcoded key — `user_id=1, notification_type='booking_confirmed', channel='email'` — against `idx_unp_user_type_channel` (unique on user_id + notification_type + channel, `app/notifications/models.py:381-382`). `users_id=1` EXISTS in the persistent shared `afcon360_test` DB (`SELECT count(*) FROM users WHERE id=1` → 1), so the INSERT passes the FK and succeeds ONCE, then every later run collides on the committed row (`duplicate key value violates unique constraint "idx_unp_user_type_channel"`). The function-scoped `db_session` teardown `rollback()` is a **no-op after an explicit commit** (`tests/conftest.py:253-259`), so committed UNP rows persist across runs. Exact failures on the current DB: `TestUserNotificationPreferenceModel::test_preference_creation`, `TestPreferenceService::test_get_preferences`, `TestPreferenceService::test_is_allowed_when_disabled`, `TestPreferenceService::test_get_enabled_channels`, `TestNotificationIntegration::test_preference_links_to_user_and_notification`. The `test_user_controls.py` FK-violation symptom documented earlier was superseded once `users.id=1` existed in the persistent DB (that test now passes).
- **MINIMAL CHANGE (TEST-ONLY):** `tests/notifications/test_models.py` — added a UUID-based `_make_user(db_session, tag)` helper (matches the established `test_transport_passengers._user` / `test_assign_revoke_org_role._make_user` family pattern) and routed the 5 preference tests' `UserNotificationPreference` inserts + service calls + assertions through a per-test unique `user.id` instead of the hardcoded `1`. No production code, no model/constraint change, no migration, no assertion weakened, no semantics changed, no DB-row deletion as workaround, no NotificationService/enum changes.
- **Verification:** the previously-failing 5 run individually → **5 passed**; full `tests/notifications/test_models.py` → **36 passed / 0 failed**, run TWICE consecutively → green both times (proves DB-pollution isolation: each run's UUID users never collide with prior committed rows); full `tests/notifications/` regression → **65 passed / 0 failed** (was 60 passed / 5 failed); startup `python -c "from app import create_app; app=create_app(); print('STARTUP_OK')"` → `STARTUP_OK`, exit 0. DB after verification: `users.id=1` still 1 row, UNP `(1,booking_confirmed,email)` still exactly 1 row; only the new unique-keyed per-run UNP rows accumulate (21 total UNP across the run history), each under a distinct UUID user — no collision possible.
- **Owner/area:** tests / notifications – user preferences (DB-pollution family)
- **Links:** tests/notifications/test_models.py, app/notifications/models.py:372-382 (UserNotificationPreference + idx_unp_user_type_channel), tests/conftest.py:253-259 (db_session teardown), BACKLOG:732-742,1055, AGENTS.md §9, §12.1, §19.3, §23, §38, §39

## Transport license-plate collision (stage5t3, DB-pollution family) — RESOLVED
- **Status:** RESOLVED (2026-09-09, dedicated Transport license-plate collision seat node) — root cause re-proven, minimal test-side fix applied, collision eliminated (see Resolution below). Historical evidence preserved above.
- **Root cause (TEST/FIXTURE DEFECT, same accumulation family):** the four transport test files generate vehicle plates as `f"UG{uuid.uuid4().hex[:4].upper()}"` — only 16 bits of entropy — and COMMIT vehicle rows into the shared persistent `afcon360_test` DB (passengers.py:72; stage5t3.py:190,543; stage5t4.py:185,248; coordination_contract.py:199,400). The committed pool has accumulated to 539 rows; once a new insert draws an existing plate it fails with `IntegrityError: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "transport_vehicles_license_plate_key"`. Reproduced: `test_t5t3_06_multiple_attendees_independent_reservations` passes ALONE, fails inside the 4-file set. This is the same pattern this node just fixed for emails — a NEW defect in `tests/test_transport_stage5t3.py`, recorded for its own node (do NOT fold into this node).
- **Suggested next node:** widen the identifier (e.g. full `uuid4().hex` or `hex[:8]`) in the committing test vehicle helpers, or otherwise guarantee per-run uniqueness while keeping DB truth; then run the 4-file reconciliation set to confirm green.
- **Links:** tests/test_transport_stage5t3.py:190,543 (failing test `t5t3_06`), tests/test_transport_stage5t4.py:185,248, tests/test_transport_coordination_contract.py:199,400, tests/test_transport_passengers.py:72, AGENTS.md §9
- **Owner/area:** tests / module isolation integration
- **Links:** tests/test_module_integration.py, app/utils/module_toggle_service.py:62-81, app/utils/module_guard.py:57-68, app/middleware/reload_modules.py:7-16, app/events/guest_coordination_service.py:109-112,751-752,885, tests/conftest.py:142-199, Stage 5-1 entry (BACKLOG:825-826), AGENTS.md §28, §9, §34, §38, §39, §21.1
- **RESOLUTION (dedicated seat node, 2026-09-09):**
  - **Classification:** TEST/FIXTURE DEFECT (DB-pollution accumulation family) — production vehicle uniqueness is correct; the failure is committed test rows colliding with each other.
  - **Re-proven (do NOT assume it was fixed):** dead-plate census on the persistent `afcon360_test` DB — `transport_vehicles` = 642 rows, distinct = 642, of which **588 are test-generated plates exactly in the `UG{4 hex}` 16-bit namespace (space = 65536)**. ORM duplicate-insert probe (mirror of the test fixture, rolled back → zero persistent change) raised `IntegrityError: (psycopg2.errors.UniqueViolation) duplicate key value violates unique constraint "transport_vehicles_license_plate_key"` / `DETAIL: Key (license_plate)=(UGED1B) already exists.`. So any fresh 16-bit draw that matches a committed plate aborts that test's commit → test failure; probability grows monotonically as rows accumulate (function-scoped `db_session` teardown `rollback()` does NOT undo commits — same accumulation family as the email/UNP collision fixes).
  - **Proof status:** prior live reproduction (t5t3_06 passes alone, fails inside the 4-file set) recorded above; this node's own pre-fix 4-file run did NOT re-hit the stochastic collision (probability per insert ≈ pool/65536 ≈ 0.9%), so the mechanism is confirmed deterministically via census + ORM IntegrityError proof rather than a repeat live failure.
  - **MINIMAL CHANGE (test-only, 7 one-line sites):** widened the test plate generator `f"UG{uuid.uuid4().hex[:4].upper()}"` → `f"UG{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8].upper()}"` (32-bit space = 4.29e9) in every COMMITTING transport vehicle helper: `tests/test_transport_stage5t3.py:190,543`, `tests/test_transport_stage5t4.py:185,248`, `tests/test_transport_coordination_contract.py:199,400`, `tests/test_transport_passengers.py:72`. Column is `String(20)` — `UG`+8 hex = 10 chars, fits. NO removal/weakening of the DB unique constraint; NO production code; NO row deletion; census confirms the constraint is still enforced (distinct == total; fresh duplicate insert still `IntegrityError`).
  - **Verification:** focused `test_t5t3_06_multiple_attendees_independent_reservations` → **1 passed**; full `tests/test_transport_stage5t3.py` → **5 passed**; reconciliation set `stage5t3 + stage5t4 + coordination_contract + passengers` → **42 passed (46s)**, run TWICE consecutively → **42 passed both times**; DB after runs: 642 total / 642 distinct (only legitimate new committed rows — never fewer, never duplicated); startup `python -c "from app import create_app; app=create_app(); print('STARTUP_OK')"` → `STARTUP_OK`, exit 0.
  - **Same-pattern observation (NOT changed):** `tests/test_stage4b5_transport.py:77,96` uses `UAY`+`hex[:4]` but its vehicles are flush-only (NEVER committed — 0 UAY rows in the persistent DB), so it is outside the committed accumulation family; left untouched per minimal scope. `tests/_diag_lock_pytest*.py` are untracked temp diagnostic files — ignored.
  - **Unrelated failures:** none encountered in the transport reconciliation suite during this node. The prior email-collision test fixes remain uncommitted in the working tree and are NOT attributed to this node; `tests/test_transport_stage5t4.py` also carries pre-existing uncommitted org-booking/email-collision edits from the prior node — untouched here.

## F-19 — Wallet withdraw API passes unsupported `user_id` kwarg → every withdraw 500s
- **Status:** Done
- **Resolved:** 2026-09-09
- **Raised:** 2026-09-09
- **Resolution (STAGE 6-2B.1, authorized wallet repair node):** Caller-side minimal fix in `app/wallet/api/wallet_api.py::withdraw` only. Proven root cause: `WalletService.withdraw(account_id: str, amount, currency, client_request_id, metadata, destination_type, destination_details, payment_method, payment_provider, tx_type, actor_id, agent_on_behalf, system_initiated)` (wallet_service.py:758-774) has NO `user_id` parameter; the API passed `user_id=` → `TypeError`, swallowed by a generic except → 500 `INTERNAL_ERROR` on every withdrawal. Every other withdraw caller (events payment_service.py:367, events/services.py:1615, routes.py:1153, agent_cashout_service.py:69, transport payment_service.py:86, wallet_service.py:1428) already uses `account_id=` correctly; deposit has a `user_id` alias (wallet_service.py:538) which is why only withdraw broke.
  - **Fix:** keep `internal_user_id = assert_internal_id(current_user.id)`; resolve the account server-side via `service.account_repo.get_by_user_id(internal_user_id, validated['currency'])`; if `None` → `raise WalletNotFoundError(wallet_ref=f"user:{internal_user_id}:{validated['currency']}")` → caught → 404; then `service.withdraw(account_id=str(account.id), ...)` with all existing kwargs preserved. No client-controlled ids accepted; `_public_wallet_data` (wallet_api.py:282-291) still strips `user_id`; service retains its own ownership check (defense in depth). WalletService signature, ledger, idempotency, and audit invariants untouched.
  - **Verified:** NEW `tests/wallet/test_withdraw_api.py` (11 tests, `TestWithdrawApiContract`) — RED (pre-fix) 7 failed/4 passed (500 on happy/no-account/wrong-currency/insufficient/idempotency/authz/dual-ID; guards passed); GREEN (post-fix) 11 passed. Regressions: `tests/wallet/test_ledger_concurrency.py` + `tests/wallet/test_payment_identity.py` + `tests/test_payment_flow.py` = 40 passed / 2 skipped; `tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` still exactly the pre-existing 7 failed / 27 passed (`uuid = integer`, independent, deferred). `python -c "from app import create_app"` STARTUP_OK. GATE: `flask db current` == `flask db heads` == `f1658aba3410` (single head, NO migration); only `app/wallet/api/wallet_api.py` (one hunk) + the new test file changed by this node; pre-existing dirty tree untouched.
- **Context:** `app/wallet/api/wallet_api.py` `withdraw()` calls `service.withdraw(user_id=..., amount=..., currency=..., client_request_id=..., ...)` at the `/api/wallet/withdraw` route, but `WalletService.withdraw(account_id, amount, currency, client_request_id, ...)` (app/wallet/services/wallet_service.py:759-774) has NO `user_id` parameter (only `account_id`). Result: `TypeError` → 500 for **every** withdrawal attempt. `deposit()` is NOT affected — its service signature includes `user_id: Optional[int] = None` alias (wallet_service.py:538). Discovered during STAGE 6-2A PROVE while building the F-04 leak-proof test (withdraw test written CONDITIONAL, asserting only on a 200).
- **Owner/area:** wallet / payments (CRITICAL)
- **Links:** app/wallet/api/wallet_api.py:~466-584 (call site ~530, fixed), app/wallet/services/wallet_service.py:759-774 (withdraw) and :538 (deposit alias), app/wallet/repositories/account_repository.py:50-77 (`get_by_user_id`, used by fix), tests/wallet/test_withdraw_api.py (NEW, 11 tests), AGENTS.md §18.1, §34, §12.1

## F-20 — Transport REST layer references non-existent `app.core.serializers` / `app.core.validators` → GETs 500 on any real row
- **Status:** Done
- **Resolved:** 2026-09-09
- **Raised:** 2026-09-09
- **Resolution (STAGE 6-2A.1, authorized repair node):** Implemented the minimal missing modules as OPTION (a):
  - `app/core/serializers.py` — `ModelSerializer.serialize(obj, include, exclude) -> dict`: iterates `inspect(obj).mapper.column_attrs`; always excludes the entity's own internal `id` (`RESERVED_INTERNAL_KEYS`); honors `include`/`exclude` lists; `_json_safe` coerces enum→value, datetime/date/time→isoformat, Decimal→str, uuid→str, dict/list pass-through, unknown→`str()`; skips keys on `AttributeError`/`DetachedInstanceError`. FK `*_id` columns remain serialized (existing admin surface) — deferred hardening to a transport public-ID node.
  - `app/core/validators.py` — `validate_coordinates(lat, lng)` wraps the existing `TransportValidators.validate_coordinates` (app/utils/validators.py:620, returns bool) and raises the canonical `ValidationError` on invalid; `validate_setting_value(value, data_type, allowed_values, validation_rules)` mirrors `SettingsService._validate_setting_value` semantics (boolean/integer/decimal/string/json normalization, allowed_values membership, min/max, min_length/max_length/pattern) and returns the normalized value or raises `ValidationError`.
  - Verified: admin/owner booking list+detail now return 200 with JSON-safe payloads; `to_dict()` never leaks internal `id`; `TransportSetting` construction/validation and `DriverProfile.update_location` coordinate contract work. Focused suite `tests/test_stage62a1_transport_serialization.py` 12 passed; F-18a suite 25 passed (1 deselected = documented session-cookie flake, below); transport stage suites + front page 8 passed; accommodation+payment subset 16 passed; `python -c "from app import create_app"` STARTUP_OK. ## F-01 — `WalletService.get_balance(account.id)` class-style misuse (instance method + wrong ID type) → wallet checkout 500 / silent-zero org balance
- **Status:** Done
- **Resolved:** 2026-09-09
- **Raised:** 2026-09-09
- **Resolution (STAGE 6-2B.2, authorized wallet boundary repair node):** four affected sites no longer call `WalletService.get_balance(account.id)`; all use the canonical account-oriented primitive `LedgerRepository().get_balance(account_uuid, currency) -> Decimal`:
  - `app/accommodation/routes.py:2100` (guest_checkout wallet pre-check) — inline TypeError→500 removed; Decimal compare against `charge_amount` with currency passed (`property_obj.currency`); hold-release + flash redirects preserved.
  - `app/accommodation/services/payment_processors/wallet_processor.py:42` — charge() now evaluates `LedgerRepository().get_balance(account.id, currency) < amount` and short-circuits `(False, None, "Insufficient ...")` before `MarketplaceService.charge_guest`; previously crashed at the balance line → wallet payments always failed.
  - `app/identity/routes.py:379` (org wallet page) — `LedgerRepository().get_balance(wallet.id, wallet.currency)`; no-wallet redirect preserved.
  - `app/identity/models/organisation.py:331` (`wallet_balance` property) — `LedgerRepository().get_balance(account.id, account.currency)`; no-account → `Decimal('0')` preserved.
  - **Residual boundary gap closed (this node's add-on):** the two accommodation sites resolved the account with `AccountModel.query.filter_by(user_id=...).first()` — currency-agnostic. PROVEN RED: a USD-funded guest charged in UGX received a false `"Insufficient wallet balance. Available: 0, Required: 200"` (wrong-currency evaluation) instead of failing as "no UGX wallet account". Repaired with the canonical user-oriented resolver `WalletService.get_wallet_by_user_id(user_id, currency)` (wallet_service.py:396 staticmethod, USER-only, currency-matching) at wallet_processor.py:38 and accommodation/routes.py:2094. App enforces one wallet per user (`get_or_create_account`, routes.py:111+), so the fix makes the currency evaluation honest and fails safe.
  - No change to `WalletService.get_balance()`, ledger logic, AccountModel, ownership/authorization, or any wallet module; no client-supplied IDs.
- **Verified:** NEW persistent `tests/wallet/test_f01_balance_boundary.py` (11 tests: root-cause shape guards — class-style `WalletService.get_balance(account.id)` TypeError + LedgerRepository().get_balance Decimal primitive + currency resolver contract; WalletProcessor.charge sufficient/insufficient-short-circuit/no-account/wrong-currency→not-found/single-UGX-wallet success, all with `MarketplaceService.charge_guest` patched; org `wallet_balance` funded→Decimal + no-account→0; org wallet page HTTP GET shows `UGX 7,500` for a funded UGX wallet with `can_manage_wallet` patched). First (pre-fix) run: 2 failed/8 passed — the wrong-currency test failed exactly as the residual defect predicted; org-page failure was a test-harness detached-instance bug (fixed). Post-fix: **11 passed**. Regressions: `tests/wallet` → **56 passed / 2 skipped**; `test_accommodation_booking.py` + `test_accommodation_checkout_processes.py` → **36 passed**; `test_org9_public_boundaries.py` + `test_org10_adversarial_cross_property.py` + `test_payment_flow.py` + `test_module_integration.py` → **50 passed**. `python -c "from app import create_app; app=create_app(); print('STARTUP_OK')"` → STARTUP_OK. GATE: `flask db current` == `flask db heads` == `f1658aba3410` (single head, NO migration); node diff = wallet_processor.py + accommodation/routes.py (balance hunks only) + new test file + this BACKLOG entry/thread_state; pre-existing dirty tree preserved. Note: the four `LedgerRepository()` balance-line repairs were discovered already present in the uncommitted working tree (matching the 6-1B F-01 repair rule) and were verified + completed here; `tests/_probe_f01_balance.py` is an untracked concurrent-actor probe, left untouched.
- **Context:** `WalletService.get_balance(self, user_id)` (wallet_service.py:424) is an INSTANCE, user-oriented method expecting the internal BIGINT user id via `assert_internal_id` (id_validator.py:6) + ownership check vs `current_user`; the affected sites passed the Account UUID (account.id) in class style → `TypeError` on the accommodation paths and a silently-zeroed balance on the identity/org display paths (broad `except Exception`). Correct primitives: account-oriented `LedgerRepository().get_balance(account_uuid, currency)`; user-orientation `WalletService().get_balance(current_user.id)`. F-01 was classified P0 in 6-1B §E and queued in 6-1C §K before 6-2B.2. `app/wallet/models` untouched (CRITICAL §18.1).
- **Owner/area:** wallet / accommodation payments / identity org display (CRITICAL)
- **Links:** app/wallet/services/wallet_service.py:424 (get_balance, unchanged), :396 (get_wallet_by_user_id canonical resolver), app/wallet/repositories/ledger_repository.py:39 (get_balance(account_id,currency)), app/accommodation/routes.py:2092-2104 + app/accommodation/services/payment_processors/wallet_processor.py:38-44 (fixed balance gates), app/identity/routes.py:377-381 + app/identity/models/organisation.py:321-334 (org display via LedgerRepository), tests/wallet/test_f01_balance_boundary.py (NEW, 11 tests), AGENTS.md §18.1, §12.1, §9

## Pre-existing — anonymous auth boundary 500s: `auth_routes.login` endpoint does not exist (BuildError)
- **Status:** Done — Resolved: 2026-09-10 (BACKLOG:1179 auth-boundary hardening node; RED→GREEN proof in tests/test_backlog1179_auth_security.py [D1])
- **Raised:** 2026-09-09
- **Context:** The auth blueprint is registered as `auth_bp` (`app/auth/routes.py:23`, endpoint prefix `auth.`), but `login_manager.login_view` (`app/extensions.py:20`) and every anonymous-path redirect in `app/auth/decorators.py` (:261, :310, :368, :407, :449, :476, :518) and `app/admin/owner/decorators.py` (:24, :101) call `url_for("auth_routes.login", ...)`. NO blueprint named `auth_routes` exists → `flask.BuildError("auth_routes.login")` → **500** for every anonymous request to any `@login_required`/`@admin_required`/`@owner_only`/`@permission_required` view (no 302 login redirect; no 401/403). Verified during STAGE 6-2A.1: isolated diagnostic showed `_get_current_user()` → `None`, `url_for("auth_routes.login", next="/")` → `BuildError`, response 500. `app/auth/routes.py` is unmodified; the F-20 repair tests keep the denial assertion (`!= 200`) with this documented.
- **What needs to happen:** Authorized auth-wiring node (BEHAVIORAL/HIGH_RISK — touches identity/auth boundaries): rename `auth_routes.login` → actual endpoint `auth.login` across `app/extensions.py`, `app/auth/decorators.py`, `app/admin/owner/decorators.py` (and audit `login_view` target). This will convert the anonymous 500s into the intended 302 login redirects. Requires careful handling of existing anonymous flows; do NOT do silently.
- **Resolved by (2026-09-10):** All 10 live code refs renamed `auth_routes.login` → `auth.login` — app/extensions.py:20, app/auth/decorators.py (:261,:310,:368,:407,:449,:476,:518), app/admin/owner/decorators.py (:24,:101) — plus doc samples in app/admin/owner/settings.md. Verified by the new RED→GREEN test `test_d1_anonymous_guarded_route_redirects_to_login` (anonymous GET /monitor now 302 → /login; was HTTP 500 BuildError at decorators.py:261); startup `endpoint_validator` clean; onboarding/session/MFA regression green (see BACKLOG:1179 report).
- **Owner/area:** auth / identity boundary
- **Links:** app/auth/routes.py:23, app/extensions.py:20, app/auth/decorators.py:407 (and siblings), app/admin/owner/decorators.py:24,101, tests/test_backlog1179_auth_security.py (D1), tests/test_stage62a1_transport_serialization.py (denial note), evidence: `url_for` BuildError diagnostic

## Transport settings — REST create/import assign `value` before `data_type`, construction-time validation skipped
- **Status:** Not started (pre-existing; documented during F-20 repair)
- **Raised:** 2026-09-09
- **Context:** `TransportSetting.validate_value` (`@validates('value')`) reads `self.data_type`/`self.allowed_values`/`self.validation_rules` at assignment time. The REST create endpoint (`app/transport/api/settings_routes.py:111-125`), bulk import (`app/transport/services/settings_service.py:905-915`), and the seed path (`app/transport/models.py:2340`) all pass `value` BEFORE the metadata kwargs, so the validator fires with `data_type=None` and silently skips validation — untrusted input can be created with 201; validation only occurs on update of an existing row (metadata already populated) or via `SettingsService._validate_setting_value`. F-20's `validate_setting_value` works correctly when metadata precedes `value` (the model's intended validated update path; test helpers mirror that). This is a model-usage order gap, not a validator defect.
- **What needs to happen:** Authorized BEHAVIORAL transport node: decide whether creation should also validate (pre-validate in `settings_routes.post` / import path, or reorder kwargs / set metadata before value) and add negative tests.
- **Owner/area:** transport settings API
- **Links:** app/transport/api/settings_routes.py:111-125, app/transport/services/settings_service.py:905-915, app/transport/models.py:1783,2340, tests/test_stage62a1_transport_serialization.py (helper docstring)

## Test infra — session-scoped `client` cookie leak breaks anonymous `test_transport_front_page` after login-bearing suites
- **Status:** RESOLVED (2026-09-10) — TEST DEFECT/infrastructure; no production change, no migration
- **Raised:** 2026-09-09
- **Context:** `client` is a SESSION-scoped fixture (tests/conftest.py:246-247), so Flask session cookies persist across tests. `tests/test_transport_stage5t3.py:583-584` logs a user in via `sess["_user_id"]`; any later test using the same `client` without clearing the session is treated as authenticated. Reproduced: `pytest tests/test_transport_stage5t3.py tests/test_transport_stage5t4.py tests/test_transport_front_page.py` → `test_transport_front_page_renders_anonymous` FAILS (`assert 'Log in to view your rides' in body`) because `home()` (app/transport/routes.py:187) sees `current_user.is_authenticated == True` and skips the anonymous CTA. File `tests/test_transport_front_page.py` is UNCOMMITTED WIP (untracked). Passes in isolation. NOT caused by STAGE 6-2A (which does not touch web transport `home()` or session handling).
- **What needs to happen:** In `tests/test_transport_front_page.py` anonymous tests, clear `session['_user_id']/['user_id']/['_fresh']` first (same pattern applied to `test_book_accommodation_requires_auth` in `tests/test_attendee_accommodation_booking.py`, see BACKLOG:1030) and commit the file.
- **Resolution (2026-09-10):** MINIMAL CHANGE (test-only) — reproduced the failure first with `pytest tests/test_transport_stage5t3.py tests/test_transport_front_page.py` → 1 failed / 12 passed (`test_transport_front_page_renders_anonymous` asserted the anonymous CTA but the leaked login cookie made `current_user.is_authenticated` True). Applied the repo's established session-clear pattern verbatim (`test_attendee_accommodation_booking.py:684-688`): each of the 5 shared-`client` (anonymous) tests in `tests/test_transport_front_page.py` (renders_anonymous, no_fake_rating_or_stats, services_come_from_enum, pane_renders, no_broken_service_detail_link) now pops `_user_id`/`user_id`/`_fresh` from the session first, so they start anonymous regardless of run order. No production code touched; the file's own authenticated tests use their own `app.test_client()` so they are unaffected. **Verification:** `tests/test_transport_front_page.py` → 8 passed; combined repro `stage5t3 + front_page` → 13 passed; full reported combination `stage5t3 + stage5t4 + front_page` → **17 passed**. No migration/schema change. File remains untracked WIP (not committed — commit deferred to user).
- **ROOT-CAUSE fix (2026-09-11, TI-1):** the per-file session-clear pattern above was a workaround for the underlying defect — the conftest `client` fixture is SESSION-scoped, so Flask session cookies (including Flask-Login `_user_id`) persist across every test that uses the shared client. TI-1 changed the fixture to `scope='function'` (tests/conftest.py), the canonical isolation fix: every test now gets a fresh client with an empty session cookie jar. The front_page anonymous tests' explicit session-clears remain harmless no-ops. Root-cause evidence: `tests/test_stage62a_transport_auth_public_id.py::test_booking_detail_requires_login` failed in-sequence (200 instead of non-200) because the prior test's `_login()` persisted in the shared cookie; with the function-scoped client the full stage62a suite passes 26/26, and the combined transport gate (stage62a+stage62a1+geographic+front_page) passes 81/81.
- **Owner/area:** tests / transport front page
- **Links:** tests/conftest.py:246-247, tests/test_transport_stage5t3.py:583-584, tests/test_transport_front_page.py:20-47, app/transport/routes.py:187, BACKLOG:1030

## FUTURE IMPLEMENTATION — AFCON360 Incident & Failure Management / Dead-Letter Operations
- **Status:** FUTURE IMPLEMENTATION — NOT STARTED
- **Classification:** FUTURE IDEA / OPERATIONAL ARCHITECTURE / NOT CURRENT DEFECT — explicitly OUT of the active defect-correction queue. DO NOT implement until a future authorized design node.
- **Raised:** 2026-09-09
- **Origin story:** This idea arose during the September 2026 backlog-clearing work while investigating the dead-letter alert path. The immediate issue turned out to be a test-side dangling patch target (`tests/test_dead_letter_alert.py:37` patching non-existent `app.transport.services.sms_service` instead of `app.notifications.sms_service`) rather than a broken production dead-letter mechanism (see entry above; also `tests/hooks_web_unit_tests.py` broken `app.tasks.webhook_processor.db` mock target, BACKLOG:1205; and the `NotificationService.send_email` repair, BACKLOG:1173). The investigation nevertheless exposed a broader operational question: **How should AFCON360 systematically capture important failures, preserve evidence, notify administrators, support investigation, and connect confirmed failures to engineering backlog work?** The team discussed a future system that would treat failures as operational incidents rather than allowing important failures to disappear into ordinary logs. This proposal came from a real operational investigation, not an abstract feature request.
- **Context (why proposed):** AFCON360 already has mechanisms that can encounter failed processing, notifications, webhooks, retries, and other exceptional conditions, but there is no single, durable operational system whose purpose is to help system administrators answer: What failed? How often? Where did it fail? Who/what was affected? Is this expected business rejection or an actual system failure? Has someone investigated it? Was the root cause confirmed? Has the issue been fixed and verified? The motivation is NOT to make the application "hide errors" — it is to ensure important failures become **visible, grouped, traceable, actionable, and eventually closed** rather than disappearing into logs.
- **Proposed future concept (tentative working name: AFCON360 Incident & Failure Management (IFM) — name provisional, may change at design time):** Conceptual lifecycle: `SYSTEM EVENT / FAILURE → CAPTURE → CLASSIFY → DEDUPLICATE / GROUP → ALERT / ROUTE → ADMIN INVESTIGATION → ROOT CAUSE → REMEDIATION → VERIFICATION → CLOSE`. Must distinguish: (a) Expected business outcomes (insufficient funds, invalid booking, unavailable room, unauthorized action, expired token — not necessarily incidents); (b) Unexpected application failures (unhandled exception, broken service contract, missing module, database constraint failure, unexpected TypeError — may become incidents); (c) Infrastructure failures (Redis unavailable, DB connection exhaustion, worker unavailable, external provider timeout — may become incidents by severity/recurrence); (d) Security events (repeated unauthorized access, suspicious patterns, repeated auth failures — may require a separate security workflow even if represented in the same system).
- **Dead-letter concept:** A dead letter = a piece of work attempted, not successfully processed, reaching a state where normal automatic processing should stop and an operator should investigate. Typical path: `Webhook / Job / Notification → processing attempt → failure → retry → retry → still failing → DEAD LETTER → ADMIN ALERT → INVESTIGATION`. Dead-letter handling must preserve the failed item and its diagnostic context rather than silently discarding it.
- **Future incident record (design consideration, not all fields required initially):** Incident ID (stable operational reference); Severity (Critical/High/Medium/Low); Category (Application/Infrastructure/Security/Integration/Background job); Module (Wallet/Events/Accommodation/Transport/Notifications/Auth/etc.); Operation; First seen; Last seen; Occurrence count; Correlation/request ID; Affected resource (booking, transaction, webhook, user, etc.); Error class; Error message; Stack trace (access-controlled); Status (New/Investigating/Mitigated/Resolved/Closed); Assigned administrator; Root cause; Remediation; Verification evidence.
- **IMPORTANT DESIGN PRINCIPLE — GROUP REPEATED FAILURES:** Must NOT create hundreds of separate incidents for the same underlying problem (e.g. 500× "Redis connection refused" → one INC with occurrences=500, first/last seen, affected modules). Future design should consider fingerprinting, error signatures, correlation IDs, time windows, deduplication, occurrence counters.
- **Admin experience (future sketch):** Dashboard with counts by severity + recent incident list; incident detail answering: WHAT HAPPENED? WHEN? WHERE? HOW MANY TIMES? WHO/WHAT WAS AFFECTED? WHAT DID THE SYSTEM TRY? WHAT FAILED? WHAT HAS BEEN DONE? IS IT FIXED?
- **Relationship to BACKLOG.md:** The future system must NOT automatically replace the engineering backlog. Proposed flow: `RUNTIME FAILURE → INCIDENT → ADMIN INVESTIGATION → CONFIRMED ENGINEERING DEFECT → BACKLOG ITEM → ENGINEERING WORK → FIX → TEST/VERIFY → INCIDENT CLOSED`. Incident (what happened operationally) and backlog item (what engineering work needs doing) remain separate concepts that may reference each other.
- **CRITICAL SAFETY RULE:** The future system must NOT automatically: modify application source code; create migrations; change DB schema; alter business rules; disable security controls; alter wallet balances; retry dangerous financial operations indefinitely; mark incidents resolved merely because the error disappeared temporarily; create backlog items without appropriate deduplication/review. An administrator or authorized workflow must remain responsible for declaring root cause and closure.
- **SPECIAL FINANCIAL RULE:** Wallet/ledger/payments/refunds/escrow/withdrawals/settlement failures must preserve forensic evidence and transaction identifiers. The incident system must be **observational and operational**, never a second financial engine — it must never directly "repair" financial state merely because an error occurred. (AGENTS.md §18.1.)
- **Possible future integrations (investigate at design time; do not assume all required — first inventory what AFCON360 already has):** Flask application error handling; structured logging; webhook processor/dead-letter handling (`app/tasks/webhook_processor.py`); Celery/background tasks; NotificationService; Redis; database errors; external payment/provider failures; authentication/security events; health monitoring; administrator notifications.
- **Future design questions (left for the authorized design phase):** (1) What qualifies as an incident? (2) What qualifies as a dead letter? (3) Which failures remain ordinary application errors? (4) How are incidents fingerprinted/grouped? (5) What severity model? (6) Which admins get which alerts? (7) What is safe to expose in the dashboard? (8) Retention period? (9) Sensitive-data/credential protection? (10) Incident↔backlog linkage? (11) How is root cause proven? (12) Resolution vs closure? (13) How are financial incidents treated differently? (14) What happens when the incident system itself is unavailable?
- **What needs to happen (ONLY when a future node is authorized):** SPECIFICATION node first per AGENTS.md §6 (BEHAVIORAL/ARCHITECTURAL/HIGH_RISK — touches wallet-adjacent observability, security events, admin authority): answer the design questions, define incident/dead-letter qualification, severity model, grouping strategy, retention, access control, and backlog linkage; then plan before any implementation. Until then: NO database tables, NO migrations, NO incident models, NO logging/NotificationService/webhook/Celery/Redis/admin-UI/error-handler/dead-letter changes, NO automatic backlog integration.
- **Owner/area:** operations / platform architecture (future)
- **Links:** `app/tasks/webhook_processor.py:434-521` (dead-letter alert path), `app/notifications/sms_service.py:262`, `app/transport/services/notification_service.py`, `tests/test_dead_letter_alert.py`, `tests/test_process_webhook_dead_letter.py`, `tests/hooks_web_unit_tests.py`, AGENTS.md §6, §17, §18.1, §29

## FUTURE IMPLEMENTATION — Deeper Transaction-Boundary Cleanup for Concurrent Booking Critical Section
- **Status:** FUTURE IMPLEMENTATION — NOT STARTED
- **Classification:** FUTURE IDEA / ARCHITECTURAL HARDENING / NOT CURRENT DEFECT — explicitly OUT of the active defect-correction queue. The existing concurrent-booking fix (advisory lock + idempotency + SELECT FOR UPDATE) is WORKING and MUST BE RETAINED. This item is a separate, deeper architectural cleanup.
- **Raised:** 2026-09-09
- **Origin story:** During review of the concurrent last-room booking fix (`test_concurrent_last_room_booking` in `tests/test_accommodation_booking.py:716`), it was observed that the advisory-lock critical section (via `pg_advisory_xact_lock` at `booking_service.py:292`) calls `AvailabilityService.block_dates()` and `AvailabilityService.block_room_type_units()`, which each perform their own `db.session.commit()` internally (see `availability_service.py:396` and `:776`). These inner commits break the atomicity of the critical section: the advisory lock is an *xact* lock (released at transaction end), but inner commits create separate transactions, potentially releasing the lock early and exposing a race window. The current fix works because idempotency keys + SELECT FOR UPDATE + advisory lock serialize correctly in practice, but the inner commits are a latent correctness risk under high contention.
- **Current state (retained, working):**
  - `BookingService.create_booking()` acquires `pg_advisory_xact_lock(:pid)` at line 292
  - Availability check uses `with_for_update()` row locks (lines 375-380, 399-404)
  - Idempotency key prevents duplicate creation (lines 309-317)
  - `test_concurrent_last_room_booking` passes: exactly one booking created under concurrent threads
- **Deferred issue:** `block_dates()` (line 396) and `block_room_type_units()` (line 776) call `db.session.commit()`; `unblock_dates()` (line 641) and `release_room_type_blocks()` (line 807) also commit. These should ideally be part of the single outer transaction so the advisory lock protects the entire booking operation atomically.
- **What needs to happen (ONLY when a future node is authorized):** ARCHITECTURAL node per AGENTS.md §6 (HIGH_RISK — touches wallet-adjacent booking flows, inventory integrity, concurrency): refactor `AvailabilityService.block_dates()`, `block_room_type_units()`, `unblock_dates()`, `release_room_type_blocks()` to *not* commit internally, instead returning the objects/changes to be committed by the caller. Update all call sites (booking_service.py:540-563, routes.py:4887,4940, create_hold, release_hold, release_expired_holds, expire_room_holds) to manage the single outer transaction. Verify `test_concurrent_last_room_booking` and all accommodation booking tests still pass. This is a non-trivial transaction-boundary redesign — do NOT attempt in a defect-correction node.
- **Owner/area:** accommodation / booking / concurrency (future)
- **Links:** `app/accommodation/services/booking_service.py:288-292,540-563`, `app/accommodation/services/availability_service.py:348-398,740-782,609-644,785-812`, `tests/test_accommodation_booking.py:716-786`, AGENTS.md §6, §18.1

## FOUND DURING F-03b (refund/payout idempotency) — separate wallet-authentication defect

- **Status:** OPEN — DEFERRED (separate node required)

For any authenticated non-owner user it raises `AttributeError`. In `transfer` (line 1081: `if not system_initiated and from_account.user_id != current_user.id and not self._is_admin()`), this means live refund/payout transfers FROM the platform account (current_user = guest/host) currently FAIL with `'...' object has no attribute 'has_role'`, caught by `MarketplaceService`'s exception path → the money-moving refund/payout flow is effectively non-functional in production.
- **Implication for F-03b:** because the primary flow currently fails BEFORE the wallet transfer executes, the double-transfer race previously demonstrated at the service level is **latent/unreachable in live routes** until this gate is fixed. The F-03b idempotency repair (stable `client_request_id` = `booking_{id}_payout` / `booking_{id}_refund` + refund convergence) is nonetheless correct and must land first, so that when this gate is repaired the flows are BOTH functional AND idempotent.
- **What needs to happen (ONLY when a separate node is authorized):** Specification + implementation node per AGENTS.md §6/§18.1/§18.2: replace the missing `has_role` call with an approved authorization check (e.g. `has_global_role` / `role_names` / policy helper), respecting active-context rules; verify `_is_admin` semantics and that `transfer` ownership gating is unchanged; add tests proving an authenticated non-owner CANNOT transfer from an account they do not own AND that refund/payout routes succeed once authorized.
- **Owner/area:** wallet / authorization
- **Links:** `app/wallet/services/wallet_service.py:69-75,1081`, `app/identity/models/user.py` (no `has_role`), `app/accommodation/services/marketplace_service.py:192,250`, `tests/wallet/test_f03b_refund_payout_idempotency.py`, AGENTS.md §6, §17, §18.1, §18.2

## FOUND DURING F-03b — fresh-uuid charge idempotency key (duplicate-payment risk)

- **Status:** OPEN — DEFERRED (belongs to a later duplicate-payment/idempotency node, not F-03b)
