## BACKLOG:1189 — Wallet UUID/Integer identity boundary (tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py) — FINAL REPORT

- **Status:** DONE (2026-09-10) — CASE A test defect, corrected test-side; production proven correct. All mandated gates PASS (`test_wallet_authorization_limits` 34 passed; `tests/wallet` 73 passed / 2 skipped standalone; migration head untouched; startup OK; git shows only the intended test file changed by this node).
- **Node:** STAGE 6-2B.x — wallet authorization-limits UUID identity recovery (deferred item (2) from 6-2A.1 report; 6-1C §K wallet-boundary lane).

### ERROR (7 RED)
`tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` had 7 failing tests:
- `TestDailyCumulative.test_regulatory_daily_enforced`
- `TestDailyCumulative.test_ledger_daily_volume_used`
- `TestDailyCumulative.test_regulatory_monthly_enforced`
- `TestDailyCumulative.test_ledger_monthly_volume_used`
- `TestDailyCumulative.test_stored_account_monthly_volume_not_authoritative`
- `TestSingleBoundary.test_service_and_direct_share_cumulative_boundary`
- `TestFailureSemantics.test_authorization_helpers_do_not_commit`

Failure signature: `.OperationalError: ... operator does not exist: uuid = integer` from PostgreSQL — tests passed integer `account_id = 1` into `KYCLimitService.enforce_cumulative_volume` / `check_regulatory_cumulative_limits`, whose `RegulatoryVolumeCalculator` queries `LedgerEntry.account_id` (UUID) with that integer. Secondary failure: the tests patched the **dead** simulation seam `app.wallet.services.wallet_service.ledger_repository.LedgerRepository`, which is no longer on the enforcement path (the path uses `RegulatoryVolumeCalculator` ORM queries directly), so the accounts under test had no ruled-out volume and boundary expectations were wrong.

### SOURCE & ROOT CAUSE
- **Production contract (verified by reading `app/wallet/models/ledger.py`):** `AccountModel.id` is `UUID`; `LedgerEntryModel.account_id` is a UUID FK to `accounts.id`; `AccountModel.user_id` is `BigInteger` FK to `users.id`. So `user_id=1` (int) is correct, but `account_id=1` (int) is WRONG — PostgreSQL correctly refuses `uuid = integer`. Production callers pass `str(account.id)` (`WalletService._check_kyc_limits` / `_check_daily_limit` given `account_id`), so **production is correct** — this is a TEST defect (CASE A).
- Enforcement path is `KYCLimitService.enforce_cumulative_volume` / `check_regulatory_cumulative_limits` → `RegulatoryVolumeCalculator` direct ORM read (`self.db.execute(...)`). `LedgerRepository` is a dead seam on this path (old mock target removed).

### CLASSIFICATION
**CASE A — test defect; production correct.** No production code, no schema, no migration involved. The `uuid = integer` failures do NOT reproduce in production because production never passes an integer `account_id`.

### CANONICAL IDENTITY CONTRACT
`account_id` (UUID) is the canonical ledger key; `user_id` (BigInteger) is the actor FK. `RegulatoryVolumeCalculator` filters ledger volume by `account_id == UUID`. Tests must construct an account whose `id` is a `uuid.UUID`/UUID-string and pass that to the enforcement helpers; integer `1` is a `user_id`, never an `account_id`.

### CHANGE
All changes confined to `tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` (26 insertions / 42 deletions vs HEAD):
- Import `RegulatoryVolumeCalculator` and `uuid4`.
- Replaced integer `account_id=1` with `uuid4()` (or `str(uuid4())`) at every `enforce_cumulative_volume` / `check_regulatory_cumulative_limits` / `_check_daily_limit` call site in the failing tests.
- Replaced the dead `patch('...ledger_repository.LedgerRepository', return_value=mock_ledger)` seam with `patch.object(RegulatoryVolumeCalculator, 'get_daily_volume' / 'get_monthly_volume', return_value=Decimal('0'|...))`, keeping the assertion that the calculator is consulted (`assert_called_once`).
- `TestSingleBoundary.test_service_and_direct_share_cumulative_boundary` conformed: `WalletService(_check_kyc_limits)` call uses `str(uuid4())` account and patches `RegulatoryVolumeCalculator.get_daily_volume`; behaviour assertions (tier-2 daily 2,000,000 boundary; direct helper AND service path AND `enforce_cumulative_volume` share the same boundary) are preserved.
- `TestDailyCumulative.test_wallet_service_enforces_operational_daily` (in the same file) conformed to the same identity contract (UUID account) — no behavioural assertion weakened.
- No attempt to coerce UUID↔int, no `::integer` casts, no assertion weakening, no skips, no production change.

### FILES CHANGED
- `tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` — the ONLY file modified by this node.
- All other working-tree changes (BACKLOG.md, app/wallet/api/wallet_api.py, app/wallet/routes.py, app/core/serializers.py, app/core/validators.py, transport/*, accommodation/*, templates/*, many other tests/*, untracked `tests/wallet/*`, `tests/_probe_f01_balance.py`, `tests/_probe_f02_debug.py`, etc.) are pre-existing concurrent/full-state dirty work — inspected, PRESERVED, NOT touched.
- Probe files `tests/z_probe_1189.py`, `tests/z_probe2_1189.py`, `tests/z_probe3_1189.py`, `tests/z_probe4_1189.py` were created during the cross-file isolation investigation and DELETED before reporting (verified: no `z_probe_1189` remains in `git status`).

### TEST RESULTS
- **Focused gate:** `pytest tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py -q` → **34 passed, 2 warnings** in 16.44 s (re-run after probes; stable).
- **Wallet baseline gate (mandated):** `pytest tests/wallet -q` → **73 passed, 2 skipped** (10644 warnings) in 455.29 s.
- **Cross-file isolation (root-cause confirmed, see BACKLOG deferral below):** running `tests/wallet` (specifically `tests/wallet/test_f03b_refund_payout_idempotency.py`) BEFORE the auth-limits file causes 14 UN-EDITED `check_transaction_allowed`-based tests in `tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` to fail. Proven PRE-EXISTING test-isolation defect in f03b (thread-unsafe mock leak of `KYCLimitService.check_transaction_allowed`), NOT caused by this node's changes, and NOT a valid gate per the mandate (the mandated gate is `tests/wallet` standalone → 73/2 passed). Recorded as deferred (below).

### POSITIVE/NEGATIVE PATH
- **Positive:** UUID account under a tier with live limits → allowed under / raises `LimitExceededError` over the regulatory (ledger-derived) volumetric boundary; both the `check_regulatory_cumulative_limits` dict-returning path and the `enforce_cumulative_volume` raising path agree.
- **Negative:** integer as `account_id` → PostgreSQL `uuid = integer` operator error (production contract enforced; no test-side silent coercion). Non-existent UUID → clean allowed/FALSE boundary check (no crash, no leak).
- Tier-0 test (`test_tier0_cannot_perform_restricted_transaction`, un-edited) confirms the restricted-permission negative path.

### OWNERSHIP BOUNDARY
No identity/wallet schema or ownership rule changed. The tests exercise `KYCLimitService` (wallet-owned) and `WalletService._check_kyc_limits` (wallet-owned) only. Org-KYB and per-transaction precedence tests are un-edited. Server-side actor resolution (§18.1) untouched.

### REGRESSION RESULTS
- `tests/wallet` standalone → **73 passed / 2 skipped** (full wallet regression group).
- Focused file → **34 passed**.
- Migration gate: `flask db current` == `flask db heads` == `f1658aba3410` (single head, NO migration).
- Startup gate: `python -c "from app import create_app"` → **STARTUP_OK**.

- LSP diagnostics: no errors reported on `tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` during edits.

### MIGRATION GATE
- **No migration required.** `flask db current` == `flask db heads` == `f1658aba3410`. No schema/model change (test-only fix). No `sync_check_constraints.py` involvement (no CHECK-constraint change).

### STARTUP GATE

### GIT GATE

## FOUND DURING BACKLOG:1189 investigation — f03b thread-unsafe mock leak breaks later test suites (pre-existing test-isolation defect)

- **Status:** CLEARED / RESOLVED (2026-09-10) — remediated by the authorized F-03b verifier/remediation node (this entry's own "what needs to happen" was the node mandate).
- **Raised:** 2026-09-10
- **Classification:** TEST-ISOLATION DEFECT (pre-existing; independent of BACKLOG:1189's test-only UUID fix). BEHAVIORAL at the test-infrastructure level.
- **Defect:** `tests/wallet/test_f03b_refund_payout_idempotency.py` `_guarded_call` (lines 62–74) patched `KYCLimitService.check_transaction_allowed` (class attribute) from inside two concurrent worker threads running via `_run_two_threads` (worker called `_guarded_call(target)` at line 308). `unittest.mock.patch` start/stop is NOT thread-safe: while thread A's patch is active, thread B entering `_guarded_call` saves mock_A as its "original"; when A exits it restores the real method; when B exits it restores mock_A → the mock stays installed process-wide AFTER f03b finishes. The leaked mock returns `{'allowed': True, 'kyc_level': 4}` (`_guarded_call` return_value). Race-dependent/flaky (probe3 saw the leaked mock; probe4 did not).
- **REMEDIATION (this node):** rewrote `_run_two_threads` to install the `_get_account_for_user` amplified-lookup AND the three KYC/fraud mocks ONCE on the main thread around the entire thread-spawn/join block (mirroring the thread-safe pattern of `tests/wallet/test_withdraw_api.py` / `test_ledger_concurrency.py`, which patch in the main thread only). Worker threads now call `target()` directly with NO per-thread `unittest.mock.patch` start/stop. Sequential-only paths (`_guarded_call` → `_guarded`) are unchanged and remain main-thread-only. Concurrency coverage preserved: still two REAL threads, separate app/test request contexts and DB sessions per worker, barrier-synchronized, `_amplified_lookup` sleep still widens the booking-grace race window; all financial assertions unchanged. The file's existing `TestMockIsolation` class (F-03B-TI) independently asserts the real implementations are restored after every concurrent run — now deterministic.
- **Verification:** (1) `pytest tests/wallet/test_f03b_refund_payout_idempotency.py` → **5 passed** (2 warnings); (2) `tests/wallet/test_f03b_refund_payout_idempotency.py::TestMockIsolation` → **1 passed**; (3) focused combined `tests/wallet/test_f03b_refund_payout_idempotency.py tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` → **39 passed**, THREE consecutive runs green (was: first run 39, then 14 failed twice when the race decided); (4) full previously-failing combination `pytest tests/wallet tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` → **107 passed / 2 skipped**, TWO consecutive full runs green (was 14 failed); (5) auth-limits standalone → **34 passed**.
- **Production status (F-03b core):** NO production code change by this node. The working-tree `app/accommodation/services/marketplace_service.py` already carries the pre-existing repair vs HEAD: deterministic `client_request_id=f"booking_{booking_id}_payout"` / `f"booking_{booking_id}_refund"` (HEAD had `_{uuid4().hex[:12]}` suffixes), plus `refund_guest` `already_processed` convergence via `TransactionRepository.get_by_client_request_id`. Verified authority chain: `TransactionModel.client_request_id` UNIQUE (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING) → `TransactionRepository.get_or_create` (`pg_insert` + `returning`) → `WalletService.transfer` takes both account rows `FOR UPDATE` in sorted UUID order (serialization point), short-circuits on `status == COMPLETED` returning `already_processed`, else writes exactly TWO ledger entries + status COMPLETED — single financial effect under concurrent duplicate requests. F-03b concurrency assertions (exactly 1 transaction, balances move once, 2 ledger entries) PASS.
- **Owner/area:** tests / wallet / f03b test isolation
- **Links:** `tests/wallet/test_f03b_refund_payout_idempotency.py:62-130,333-381`, `tests/wallet/test_ledger_concurrency.py:149,211,279,348,462` (non-threaded, safe), `tests/wallet/test_withdraw_api.py:129` (non-threaded, safe), `app/wallet/services/kyc_limit_service.py` (`check_transaction_allowed`), `tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` (un-edited cta() tests), `app/wallet/repositories/transaction_repository.py:29-90`, `app/wallet/models/transaction.py:67-72`, `app/wallet/services/wallet_service.py:1036,1166-1177,1213`, AGENTS.md §9, §38

## DEFERRED (E/B, test isolation, NOT fixed by TH-3): geographic payload test fragile under shared-DB pollution
- Status: Done (resolved 2026-09-11) — fixed by TI test-isolation node. FINAL repair supersedes TI-1's initial `limit=100000` workaround (see Resolution).
- Resolution (2026-09-11, TI, FINAL): MINIMAL TEST-ONLY change — the test performs **transactional eligibility isolation**: within its own test transaction it marks every other driver non-eligible (`is_online`/`is_available` = False for `id != fixture`) and calls the pool at the original **bounded `limit=100`**, so the fixture driver is deterministically the only eligible row regardless of shared-DB pollution. The update is never committed; `db_session` teardown rolls it back (other suites' data untouched). TI-1's initial `limit=100000` request (listed below in Notes) was removed because it masked rather than repaired the defect and violates the isolation mandate ("do NOT bump LIMIT arbitrarily"). No production code, query ordering, or schema touched (the unordered `LIMIT` provider behavior is a documented intentional design, provider_service.py:1296-1313). Determinism evidence: RED at `limit=100` with 263 eligible drivers in the shared test DB (fixture truncated, 1 failed); GREEN with isolation at the same 263-eligible state; GEOGRAPHY file 35 passed under heavy pollution and again under a 29-eligible count; 9-suite combined transport+auth run **170 passed** in forward and reverse file order; stage62a+stage62a1 pair 38 passed; collect-only 1286 / 0 errors. Notes (historical, superseded): TI-1's `limit=100000` determinism probe (`limit=100` omitted target 2/4 runs; `limit=100000` included it 4/4 runs) remains valid evidence of the fragility.
- Defect: `TestProviderAvailabilityPayload::test_payload_carries_location_fields` (`tests/test_transport_geographic_contract.py:392-410`) asserts the freshly-created fixture driver appears in `get_available_drivers(limit=100)`. The pool selector applies `LIMIT 100` with NO `ORDER BY` (provider_service.py:1347); when the shared, session-scoped `afcon360_test` DB accumulates >=100 committed APPROVED/online/available drivers from other suites run earlier in the same pytest session, the fixture driver is truncated out of the unordered top-100 -> `len(match)==0` (1 failed / 34 passed). Reproduced + root-caused via a deleted throwaway probe (`tests/z_probe_geo_payload.py`, removed; pool 100, fresh driver absent, DB had ~100 eligible rows). Direct app-context harness proved the CONTRACT is correct on a sparse DB (`PROBE_CONTRACT_OK`: fresh eligible driver returned with canonical `current_location` + `location_updated_at`). Clean test DB (`python scripts/setup_test_db_schema.py`) → suite **35 passed**. Classification: primary **E — test infrastructure/isolation defect (shared test-DB pollution across suites)**; secondary **B-type test fragility** (`limit=100` + un-ordered pool assumption). NOT a TH-3 production defect (A).
- Defect: `TestProviderAvailabilityPayload::test_payload_carries_location_fields` (`tests/test_transport_geographic_contract.py:392-410`) asserts the freshly-created fixture driver appears in `get_available_drivers(limit=100)`. ## F-11 - Penalty idempotency key (`cancel-{id}-{time_ns()}`) - PENALTY/CANCELLATION
- **Status:** PROVEN SAFE (CASE A) 2026-09-11 - evidence-gated node complete. **NO PRODUCTION CHANGE** (prompt §9: proof-only result is a valid successful outcome).
- **Suspected defect:** `BookingService.cancel_booking()` penalty key fallback is non-deterministic: `penalty_key = idempotency_key or f"cancel-{booking.id}-{time_ns()}"` (`app/accommodation/services/booking_service.py:1741`). Both production callers (`guest_cancel_booking` routes.py:3742, `host_cancel_booking` routes.py:5243) pass NO `idempotency_key`, so the fallback fires on every call -> DB UNIQUE `idx_cancel_penalty_idempotency` and the `existing_penalty` lookup are dead as dedupe on retry.
- **Root cause (why it is NOT exploitable):** duplicate penalty creation is UNREACHABLE because cancellation is defended by four independent layers: (1) `get_cancellation_quote()` status gate - CANCELLED/REFUNDED bookings are not in `CANCELLABLE_STATUSES` (`models/booking.py:513-521,589-590`) so a retry returns before any mutation; (2) `select(...).with_for_update()` booking row lock (`booking_service.py:1622-1624`) serializes concurrent duplicates - the loser re-reads the committed CANCELLED row; (3) `BookingStateMachine` - `CANCELLED: [REFUNDED]` only, no self-transition, raises `InvalidStateTransition` -> full rollback (`state_machine/booking_states.py:69,254-258`); (4) UNIQUE `idx_cancel_penalty_idempotency` on `CancellationPenalty.idempotency_key` final backstop. CancellationPenalty is created in exactly one place and is NEVER charged (no PENDING->CHARGED/PAID transition, no penalty wallet debit reference anywhere) - the debt record is the terminal financial effect.
- **Classification:** CASE A - PRODUCTION CORRECT. Non-deterministic key = latent weakness / decorative idempotency mechanism, NOT a reachable duplicate financial effect (retry and concurrency are rejected by layers 1-3 before insert). No schema change; no migration; no production edit.
- **Evidence (untracked probe `tests/wallet/test_f11_penalty_idempotency_key.py`, patch-free):** 6 proofs PASS: (a) first cancel -> exactly 1 Penalty (amount 100.00, PENDING, key matches `cancel-{id}-{time_ns}` shape); (b) retry -> rejected, still exactly 1 penalty, key unchanged; (c) concurrent duplicate (2 real threads, barrier, separate app/request contexts + DB sessions) -> exactly 1 success + 1 rejection, exactly 1 penalty; (d) mid-transaction commit failure -> rollback leaves 0 penalties + booking stays CONFIRMED, retry converges to exactly 1; (e) two distinct bookings -> 2 penalties, distinct keys; (f) non-owner/host user -> rejected "not authorised", 0 penalties. Tests reuse the F-02/F-03 wallet fixture pattern; NO mocks used (no mock-leak surface). One intermittent f03b flake observed in an early 4-file run (f03b `with self.db.begin()` "closed transaction", 1/4) - reproduced as green standalone (6), with f02 (12), with f11 (12), and in the full 4-file rerun (52) + full wallet gate (122/2); alphabetically f11 collects AFTER f03b so it cannot affect it in-process; recorded, not F-11-attributable.
- **Verification:** f11 standalone **6 passed**; f02+f03b **12 passed**; f03b+f11 **12 passed**; 4-file (f02+f03b+f11+auth-limits) rerun **52 passed**; mandatory regression `pytest tests/wallet tests/test_wallet_authorization_limits.py` (incl. f11) **122 passed / 2 skipped**; startup `STARTUP_OK`; migrations `flask db current` == `flask db heads` == `f1658aba3410` unchanged; footprint = BACKLOG.md + untracked probe file only.
- **Owner/area:** accommodation / cancellation penalty idempotency
- **Links:** `app/accommodation/services/booking_service.py:1603-1785` (cancel_booking, penalty block 1738-1765), `app/accommodation/models/booking.py:513-521,589-590` (CANCELLABLE_STATUSES / quote gate), `app/accommodation/state_machine/booking_states.py:37-87,254-258` (VALID_TRANSITIONS / InvalidStateTransition), `app/accommodation/models/cancellation_policy.py:402-454` (unique idx_cancel_penalty_idempotency), `app/accommodation/routes.py:3718-3774,5226-5265` (callers pass no idempotency_key), `tests/wallet/test_f11_penalty_idempotency_key.py`, AGENTS.md §6, §9, §38

- **Status:** RESOLVED 2026-09-17 (fixed: added `code=None, details=None` optional parameters to all 9 exception classes in `app/utils/exceptions.py`; all ~35 call sites now pass kwargs without TypeError; regression tests `tests/test_exception_keyword_args.py` 12/12 pass; verified `python -c "from app import create_app; print('STARTUP_OK')"` and `flask db heads` == `f1658aba3410` unchanged; no D2 dispatch path modified).

App startup `STARTUP_OK` confirmed. Migration head `f1658aba3410` unchanged. No D2 dispatch path modified.

## Flask-RESTful × Flask-Login — anonymous 401 rendered as 500 (pre-existing, repo-wide)
- **Status:** RESOLVED 2026-09-17 (fixed: enhanced `unauthorized()` handler in `app/__init__.py` to check `request.path.startswith('/api/')` first and return proper JSON 401 for API requests; web routes retain flash+redirect login behavior; regression tests `tests/test_transport_restful_auth.py` key test `test_anonymous_driver_location_post_returns_401` passes; auth route security test `test_d1_anonymous_guarded_route_redirects_to_login` passes preserving web backward compatibility).
- **Raised:** 2026-09-12 (proven during TH-3-D2 FINAL EVIDENCE GATE)
- **Context:** Any `@login_required` POST/route on a **flask-restful Resource** anonymously returns HTTP 500 (`TypeError: Object of type Response is not JSON serializable`) instead of 401. Mechanism: the app login-manager `unauthorized()` handler (`app/__init__.py:664-671`) returns a `Response`; the flask-restful `Api` error path (via protected-route `@login_required`) tries to jsonify that Response and crashes. Proven on TWO endpoints: TH-3-D2 `DriverLocationResource.post` AND the non-D2 `/api/transport/drivers/me/offers/REF-1/decline` (both 500 under anonymous, CSRF disabled in tests). Affects roughly every flask-restful endpoint using `@login_required`. **Security property HOLDS:** the unauthenticated write is still denied — no data read or mutation — so the D2 gate anonymous negative rows assert `status_code >= 400` + `error` key, NOT an exact 401.
- **What needs to happen (COMPLETED):** Enhanced `unauthorized()` handler in `app/__init__.py` to check `request.path.startswith('/api/')` first and return `jsonify({"error": "Unauthorized"}), 401` for API requests, while preserving the existing flash+redirect behavior for non-API (web) routes. This narrow, additive 3-line change at the top of the handler resolves the 500→401 mismatch for all flask-restful protected endpoints without redesigning auth, without touching the login_manager, and without affecting web route login flows.
- **Resolution (2026-09-17):** Added `/api/` path check at line 667-668 in `app/__init__.py` `unauthorized()` handler: `if request.path.startswith('/api/'): return jsonify({"error": "Unauthorized"}), 401`. Verified: anonymous `DriverLocationResource.post` now returns 401 JSON instead of 500; anonymous `/api/transport/drivers/me/offers/REF-1/decline` now returns 401 JSON; web route `test_d1_anonymous_guarded_route_redirects_to_login` still passes (flash+redirect preserved); regression tests `tests/test_transport_restful_auth.py` key test passes; `tests/test_exception_keyword_args.py` 12/12 pass (Defect A also resolved). No auth redesign, no migration, no schema change.
- **Owner/area:** framework integration / flask-restful / auth

### M2 — `chk_pickup_time_future` vs `pickup_time=now()` fallback: latent `CheckViolation` (deferred schema/runtime constraint finding)
- **Status:** DEFERRED — SCHEMA/RUNTIME CONSTRAINT (recorded 2026-09-12 TH-3 NEXT-NODE AUDIT). Not fixed.
- **Context:** `app/transport/models.py` (~:897) declares `CheckConstraint('pickup_time > created_at', name='chk_pickup_time_future')`. `BookingService.create_booking` (`app/transport/services/booking_service.py:91-93`) falls back to `pickup_time = datetime.now(timezone.utc)` when `pickup_time` is missing/unparseable. If the fallback `now()` equals or precedes the row's `created_at` timestamp value at flush (clock/snapshot edge), the CHECK can fail with a `CheckViolation` on a path that is not user-facing input error.
- **Classification:** deferred schema/runtime constraint finding; latent edge on the malformed-input fallback path; non-blocking.
- **Links:** `app/transport/services/booking_service.py:91-93`, `app/transport/models.py` `ck_*` check constraint block (~:897).

### M3 — Web admin cancellation ownership `PermissionError` → generic flash (deferred UX/path finding)
- **Status:** DEFERRED — UX/PATH (recorded 2026-09-12 TH-3 NEXT-NODE AUDIT). Not fixed.
- **Context:** `POST /transport/admin/bookings/<id>/cancel` (`app/transport/routes.py:1235-1265`) calls `get_booking_service().cancel_booking(booking_id, user_id=current_user.id)`. `cancel_booking` (`booking_service.py:205-240`) applies an ownership guard at ~:213 and raises `PermissionError` when the actor is not the booking owner. The route's `try/except` handles `NotFoundError`/`ServiceUnavailableError`/`ValidationError`/`Exception` but NOT `PermissionError`, so a non-owner admin cancel falls to the generic `Exception` branch (rollback + "Unexpected error cancelling booking" flash). The canonical admin cancellation path (API `BookingStatusResource` POST CANCELLED, `booking_routes.py:279+`) is NOT affected — capability exists; this is a web-shortcut UX defect, not an ownership bypass.
- **Classification:** deferred UX/path finding; owner-limited web shortcut; non-blocking.
- **Links:** `app/transport/routes.py:1235-1265`, `app/transport/services/booking_service.py:205-240` (`cancel_booking`, ownership guard ~:213), `app/transport/api/booking_routes.py:279+`.

- **Context:** `connect_transport_listeners` is defined only in `app/transport/event_listeners.py:75` and is referenced NOWHERE in `app/` (dead code). The live wiring is `app/__init__.py:1228-1229` → `app/transport/listeners.py:9 register_event_listeners`, which registers no-op stubs only (logging, no Transport actions). Event-driven listener integration is therefore NOT operational. This is distinct from the operational direct contract integration (`Event → GuestCoordinationService → TransportCoordinationContract → Transport`).
- **Classification:** deferred architecture/unused-integration finding; informational; non-blocking.
- **Links:** `app/transport/event_listeners.py:75`, `app/transport/listeners.py:9`, `app/__init__.py:1228-1229`.

### M5 — Booking creation status duality: web → `PENDING_PAYMENT`, admin API → `DRAFT` (UNRECONCILED — SEVERITY TBD)
- **Status:** UNRECONCILED — SEVERITY TBD (recorded 2026-09-12 TH-3 NEXT-NODE AUDIT). Not fixed. Not called "intentional": no explicit evidence of intent was located in this audit (only the two creation paths were verified).
- **Context (facts):** (a) web `/book` (app/transport/routes.py:291-331) → `BookingService.create_booking` explicitly sets `status=BookingStatus.PENDING_PAYMENT` (`booking_service.py:120`) with `payment_status=PENDING`; (b) admin API `POST /api/transport/bookings` (`booking_routes.py:143-177`) builds `Booking(**{k: validated[k] for k in required})` WITHOUT `status` → SQLAlchemy column default `BookingStatus.DRAFT` applies (`models.py:1013`). Two creation paths therefore materialize different initial statuses.
- **Impact assessment:** pending (severity TBD) — DRAFT bookings never enter the dispatch/offer/claim flows (claim gated on CONFIRMED via `CLAIMABLE_STATUSES`); no test currently asserts the intended status of the API-created booking.
- **Classification:** UNRECONCILED — SEVERITY TBD; requires a decision (align API create to PENDING_PAYMENT + payment boundary, or document DRAFT as the deliberate staging status) before it can be closed.
- **Links:** `app/transport/api/booking_routes.py:143-177`, `app/transport/routes.py:291-331`, `app/transport/services/booking_service.py:102-127` (status at :120), `app/transport/models.py:1013`.
